Stage 3 — Sheet A (per response)

Score each response 1–5 on 4 criteria: task_decomposition, sequencing_logic, actionability, cognitive_load. 120 responses. Model identity is hidden. This page is read-only — type your scores in your own CSV, matching the ID shown on each card.

Score descriptors (manuscript Appendix D.1) — 5 is best

Task Decomposition Quality

5Tasks are clearly worded, appropriately granular, and fully complete; every necessary component of the deliverable is represented with no redundancy.
4Tasks are mostly clear and granular with minor gaps or slight over-bundling of steps.
3Tasks are adequately broken down but noticeably uneven in granularity or missing one or two components.
2Tasks are vague or inconsistently sized; significant components are missing or conflated.
1Output is not decomposed into meaningful tasks; items are too broad, too granular, or incomplete to be usable.

Sequencing Logic

5Prerequisite relationships are fully and correctly identified; foundational tasks precede their dependents, and independent sub-goals are represented as separate, non-blocking strands rather than flattened into a single chain.
4Prerequisites are mostly correct, with one or two minor errors or missed opportunities to represent independent strands separately.
3Prerequisites are partially correct but contain noticeable errors, or independent sub-goals are unnecessarily flattened into a single chain, requiring student adjustment.
2Prerequisites are largely incorrect or missing; dependent tasks are not linked to what they depend on, such that following the structure would create friction.
1No discernible prerequisite structure; tasks show no handling of dependencies.

Actionability

5All tasks are immediately executable as written; each begins with a clear action verb and specifies exactly what the student must produce or accomplish.
4Most tasks are immediately executable; one or two require minor reinterpretation before the student can begin.
3Some tasks are actionable but several require the student to plan sub-steps before starting.
2Most tasks are stated as goals or topics rather than actions; the student must significantly re-plan before executing.
1Tasks are not actionable; output reads as an outline or topic list rather than a set of executable steps.

Cognitive Load Reduction Potential

5Output completely removes ambiguity about what to do next; the student faces no residual planning decisions before beginning each task.
4Output substantially reduces ambiguity; minimal residual decision-making required before starting.
3Output reduces some ambiguity but leaves noticeable open questions the student must resolve independently.
2Output provides little decision support; the student must still perform most of the planning work themselves.
1Output adds confusion or introduces new ambiguity; provides no meaningful reduction in planning burden.

R001 S3-P2

Prompt

I need to write Chapter 1 of a research paper on social media's effects on teenagers. The paper examines how social media use affects teenagers' mental health and academic performance. Chapter 1 consists of four sections: Background of the Study, Research Objectives, Scope and Limitations, and Significance of the Study. Break this chapter down into microtasks I can execute one at a time, noting which microtasks depend on others and which can be worked on independently.

16 microtasks · 3 with no prerequisites

  1. 1
    Draft the primary research questions and specific research objectives regarding social media's effects on teenagers' mental health and academic performance.
    no prerequisites · unlocks 9, 10, 13
  2. 2
    Gather 3-5 key academic sources and statistics on the relationship between teenage social media use and mental health.
    no prerequisites · unlocks 4, 6
  3. 3
    Gather 3-5 key academic sources and statistics on the relationship between teenage social media use and academic performance.
    no prerequisites · unlocks 4, 7
  4. 4
    Create a detailed outline for the Background of the Study section, structuring the flow from broad social media trends to specific mental health and academic impacts.
    needs 2, 3 · unlocks 5, 6, 7
  5. 5
    Write the introductory paragraphs of the Background of the Study, establishing the global context of teenage social media consumption.
    needs 4 · unlocks 8
  6. 6
    Write the subsection of the Background of the Study focusing on social media's impact on teenage mental health, integrating the gathered sources.
    needs 2, 4 · unlocks 8
  7. 7
    Write the subsection of the Background of the Study focusing on social media's impact on academic performance, integrating the gathered sources.
    needs 3, 4 · unlocks 8
  8. 8
    Write the concluding paragraphs of the Background of the Study, explicitly identifying the gap in literature that this study aims to fill.
    needs 5, 6, 7 · unlocks 15
  9. 9
    Refine and format the draft Research Objectives into a formal 'Research Objectives' section, distinguishing between the main objective and specific sub-objectives.
    needs 1 · unlocks 15
  10. 10
    Define the parameters of the study's scope, including target age group, specific social media platforms, geographical location, and timeframe.
    needs 1 · unlocks 11, 12
  11. 11
    Identify potential limitations of the study, such as self-reporting biases, sample size constraints, or the correlational nature of the data.
    needs 10 · unlocks 12
  12. 12
    Draft the 'Scope and Limitations' section by combining the defined scope parameters and identified limitations into a cohesive narrative.
    needs 10, 11 · unlocks 15
  13. 13
    Identify the key stakeholders who will benefit from this research (e.g., teenagers, parents, educators, mental health professionals, policy makers).
    needs 1 · unlocks 14
  14. 14
    Draft the 'Significance of the Study' section, detailing the specific practical or theoretical contributions of the research for each identified stakeholder.
    needs 13 · unlocks 15
  15. 15
    Compile the four drafted sections (Background, Objectives, Scope and Limitations, Significance) into a single Chapter 1 document and add transitional sentences between sections.
    needs 8, 9, 12, 14 · unlocks 16
  16. 16
    Proofread Chapter 1 for academic tone, clarity, and adherence to the required citation style (e.g., APA, MLA).
    needs 15

R004 S3-P6

Prompt

I need to write the related literature section of our research paper on the effects of social media use on teenagers. I do not know where to start. Create microtasks that would help me write this section of the paper.

22 microtasks · 3 with no prerequisites

  1. 1
    Clarify the specific research questions or objectives of your paper to guide the literature review.
    no prerequisites · unlocks 2
  2. 2
    Brainstorm a comprehensive list of keywords and search terms related to 'social media,' 'teenagers,' 'effects,' and specific sub-topics (e.g., mental health, academic performance, social development).
    needs 1 · unlocks 5
  3. 3
    Identify 3-5 relevant academic databases (e.g., PubMed, PsycINFO, Scopus, Web of Science, Google Scholar) for literature search.
    no prerequisites · unlocks 5
  4. 4
    Decide on a consistent method for taking notes (e.g., digital notes, summary table, reference manager annotations) for each article.
    no prerequisites · unlocks 9, 10, 11
  5. 5
    Perform initial searches in the selected databases using the brainstormed keywords.
    needs 2, 3 · unlocks 6
  6. 6
    Review titles and abstracts of initial search results to identify potentially relevant articles.
    needs 5 · unlocks 7
  7. 7
    Download full-text PDFs of all articles deemed potentially relevant.
    needs 6 · unlocks 8
  8. 8
    Skim the introduction, methods, results, and discussion sections of downloaded articles to confirm their direct relevance to your paper's focus. Discard irrelevant ones.
    needs 7 · unlocks 9, 10, 11
  9. 9
    Read the first batch of 5-7 relevant articles thoroughly, taking detailed notes on main arguments, key findings, methodology, theoretical framework, and how each relates to your research questions, using your chosen note-taking system.
    needs 4, 8 · unlocks 10, 12
  10. 10
    Read the next batch of 5-7 relevant articles thoroughly, taking detailed notes.
    needs 4, 8, 9 · unlocks 11, 12
  11. 11
    Read all remaining relevant articles thoroughly, taking detailed notes.
    needs 4, 8, 10 · unlocks 12
  12. 12
    Review all notes to identify recurring themes, debates, theories, and gaps in the literature.
    needs 9, 10, 11 · unlocks 13
  13. 13
    Create a detailed outline for the literature review section, organizing the identified themes into logical sub-sections.
    needs 12 · unlocks 14, 15
  14. 14
    Draft the introductory paragraph for the literature review section, stating its purpose and scope.
    needs 13
  15. 15
    Draft the first sub-section of the literature review, synthesizing information from multiple sources related to that theme, ensuring proper in-text citations.
    needs 13 · unlocks 16
  16. 16
    Draft the second sub-section of the literature review, synthesizing information from multiple sources related to that theme, ensuring proper in-text citations.
    needs 15 · unlocks 17
  17. 17
    Draft all remaining sub-sections of the literature review, following the outline and synthesizing information from multiple sources.
    needs 16 · unlocks 18
  18. 18
    Draft the concluding paragraph for the literature review, summarizing key findings from the literature and highlighting how it leads to or justifies your study.
    needs 17 · unlocks 19, 21
  19. 19
    Review the entire literature review draft for overall coherence, logical flow, and smooth transitions between paragraphs and sections.
    needs 18 · unlocks 20
  20. 20
    Check that the literature review builds a clear argument or narrative that supports your paper's research questions/objectives.
    needs 19 · unlocks 22
  21. 21
    Verify all in-text citations against the reference list and ensure adherence to the required citation style.
    needs 18 · unlocks 22
  22. 22
    Proofread the entire literature review section for grammar, spelling, and punctuation errors.
    needs 20, 21

R005 S3-P3

Prompt

I need to write the Significance of the Study section for a research paper on sleep deprivation and academic performance among undergraduate students. The section needs to address significance to the academic community, to university administrators, and to student health services. Give me the most granular breakdown of microtasks possible for writing this section.

14 microtasks · 1 with no prerequisites

  1. 1
    Read and summarize key peer‑reviewed studies on the relationship between sleep deprivation and academic performance among undergraduate students.
    no prerequisites · unlocks 2, 5, 8, 13
  2. 2
    Identify and document specific gaps or under‑explored areas in the existing literature on sleep deprivation and academic outcomes.
    needs 1 · unlocks 3, 13
  3. 3
    Synthesize how the current research base advances knowledge for the academic community (e.g., theoretical insights, methodological contributions).
    needs 2 · unlocks 4, 13
  4. 4
    Draft the significance paragraph addressing the academic community, integrating the synthesized contributions from step 3.
    needs 3 · unlocks 11
  5. 5
    Review university policy documents, student handbooks, and institutional data reports on student sleep patterns and related academic support.
    needs 1 · unlocks 6, 13
  6. 6
    Identify practical implications of sleep deprivation for university administrators, including effects on retention, grading, and resource allocation.
    needs 5 · unlocks 7
  7. 7
    Draft the significance paragraph addressing university administrators, outlining the administrative implications identified in step 6.
    needs 6 · unlocks 11
  8. 8
    Examine existing student health services’ programs, resources, and records related to sleep health and wellness.
    needs 1 · unlocks 9, 13
  9. 9
    Identify gaps or unmet needs in student health services concerning sleep deprivation and its impact on student well‑being.
    needs 8 · unlocks 10, 13
  10. 10
    Draft the significance paragraph addressing student health services, highlighting the identified gaps and needed improvements from step 9.
    needs 9 · unlocks 11
  11. 11
    Combine the three significance paragraphs (from steps 4, 7, and 10) into a single, cohesive Significance of the Study section.
    needs 4, 7, 10 · unlocks 12
  12. 12
    Edit and refine the combined Significance of the Study section for clarity, conciseness, and alignment with journal guidelines.
    needs 11 · unlocks 14
  13. 13
    Verify and format all citations used in the Significance of the Study section according to the target journal’s citation style.
    needs 1, 2, 3, 5, 8, 9 · unlocks 14
  14. 14
    Finalize the Significance of the Study section and insert it into the full manuscript at the appropriate location.
    needs 12, 13

R006 S3-P4

Prompt

I am tasked with writing the methodology section for a research paper on the effects of social media on teenagers, in which we will be surveying and interviewing undergraduate students within our college. Break this down into microtasks that are simple to understand and initiate, noting which microtasks depend on others and which can be worked on independently.

19 microtasks · 1 with no prerequisites

  1. 1
    Define research objectives and research questions for the study on social media effects on teenagers.
    no prerequisites · unlocks 2, 3, 5, 7
  2. 2
    Conduct a literature review focusing on existing research about social media impacts on teenagers.
    needs 1 · unlocks 4, 5, 18
  3. 3
    Identify and select an appropriate survey methodology (e.g., cross‑sectional, online) for the study.
    needs 1 · unlocks 4
  4. 4
    Develop the survey questionnaire items based on the chosen methodology and research questions.
    needs 2, 3 · unlocks 6, 9
  5. 5
    Draft the interview guide questions aligned with the research objectives and literature findings.
    needs 1, 2 · unlocks 6, 10
  6. 6
    Submit and obtain IRB (Institutional Review Board) approval for the survey and interview instruments.
    needs 4, 5 · unlocks 7, 8, 11, 12
  7. 7
    Determine sample size and define a sampling strategy (e.g., convenience sampling of undergraduate students).
    needs 1, 6 · unlocks 8
  8. 8
    Create participant recruitment materials (flyer, email template) based on the sampling plan and IRB requirements.

Break this down into microtasks for presenting the quantitative results, discussing the qualitative themes, and connecting both back to the research questions, noting which microtasks depend on others and which can be worked on independently.

Prompt

We ran a controlled group experiment with 80 participants and found that individuals were significantly more likely to change their answer when exposed to unanimous incorrect group responses, along with several recurring themes from follow-up interviews about social pressure. Break this down into microtasks for presenting the quantitative results, discussing the qualitative themes, and connecting both back to the research questions, noting which microtasks depend on others and which can be worked on independently.

R116 S3-P6

Prompt

I need to write the related literature section of our research paper on the effects of social media use on teenagers. I do not know where to start. Create microtasks that would help me write this section of the paper.

15 microtasks · 1 with no prerequisites

    Define a list of search keywords and boolean operators related to social media use and teenagers (e.g., 'social media AND adolescents AND mental health').

R117 S3-P8

Prompt

I need to write the Results and Discussion chapter for a research paper on group conformity in decision-making among university students. We ran a controlled group experiment with 80 participants and found that individuals were significantly more likely to change their answer when exposed to unanimous incorrect group responses, along with several recurring themes from follow-up interviews about social pressure. Break this down into microtasks for presenting the quantitative results, discussing the qualitative themes, and connecting both back to the research questions, noting which microtasks depend on others and which can be worked on independently.

12 microtasks · 2 with no prerequisites

  1. 1
    Organize the experimental data, compute descriptive statistics, and create figures/tables for the quantitative results (e.g., mean percentages of answer changes across conditions).
    no prerequisites · unlocks 2
  2. 2
    Write the quantitative results section, reporting statistical tests, significance levels, and including the tables/figures created in Task 1.
    needs 1 · unlocks 3
  3. 3
    Draft the quantitative discussion, interpreting the statistical findings, relating them to prior literature on group conformity, and noting limitations.
    needs 2 · unlocks 9
  4. 4
    Transcribe all audio recordings from the follow‑up interviews using a transcription tool or service.
    no prerequisites · unlocks 5
  5. 5
    Code the interview transcripts to identify recurring themes (e.g., fear of dissent, desire for acceptance, perceived social pressure) using a systematic coding scheme.
    needs 4 · unlocks 6
  6. 6
    Summarize the identified qualitative themes in bullet points or a thematic matrix for the results section.
    needs 5 · unlocks 7
  7. 7
    Write the qualitative results section, presenting the themes and illustrative quotes, and linking them to the interview data.
    needs 6 · unlocks 8
  8. 8
    Draft the qualitative discussion, interpreting the themes in the context of social pressure theory and connecting them to the quantitative findings.
    needs 7 · unlocks 9
  9. 9
    Create an integrated discussion that weaves together the quantitative and qualitative results, highlighting convergences and divergences.
    needs 3, 8 · unlocks 10
  10. 10
    Explicitly align the integrated discussion with the study’s research questions, stating how each finding addresses the overarching aims.
    needs 9 · unlocks 11
  11. 11
    Write a concluding paragraph that summarizes the overall findings, discusses implications for theory and practice, and suggests future research directions.
    needs 10 · unlocks 12
  12. 12
    Perform a final review and edit of the entire Results and Discussion chapter for coherence, formatting, and adherence to journal guidelines.
    needs 11

R120 S3-P5

Prompt

I am writing the scope and limitations section for our research paper on peer pressure and risk-taking behavior among adolescents. We will only have high school students from our partner schools as participants. Another limitation is that we are not measuring long-term behavioral outcomes beyond the school year. Create microtasks that I can accomplish procedurally to complete this section of the paper.

15 microtasks · 8 with no prerequisites

  1. 1
    Review journal guidelines for scope and limitations section formatting.
    no prerequisites · unlocks 9, 10, 11, 12
  2. 2
    Draft a brief overview of the study's focus (peer pressure and risk-taking behavior among adolescents).
    no prerequisites · unlocks 9
  3. 3
    List and describe the participant inclusion criteria (high school students from partner schools, age range, grades).
    no prerequisites · unlocks 9, 10
  4. 4
    List and describe the participant exclusion criteria (non‑partner schools, younger/older students, etc.).
    no prerequisites · unlocks 9, 10
  5. 5
    Define the geographic and institutional scope (partner schools only).
    no prerequisites · unlocks 9, 10
  6. 6
    Define the temporal scope (one school year).
    no prerequisites · unlocks 9, 11
  7. 7
    Identify and list the primary outcomes to be measured (e.g., self‑reported peer pressure exposure, risk‑taking behavior scales).
    no prerequisites · unlocks 9, 12
  8. 8
    Identify and list any secondary outcomes (if applicable).
    no prerequisites · unlocks 9
  9. 9
    Write the first draft of the scope paragraph incorporating population, setting, timeframe, and focus.
    needs 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8 · unlocks 13
  10. 10
    Write the first draft of the limitations paragraph addressing sample generalizability (partner schools only).
    needs 1, 3, 4, 5 · unlocks 13
  11. 11

    needs 1, 6 · unlocks 13

    needs 14
